みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ 
～不動産不況は脱却の兆しなお見えず～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹  
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 中国政府が新規則を発表。住宅は予約販売から完成後販売にシフト 
中国政府は8月28日、住宅販売制度と住宅ローン管理に関する新規則を発表した。新規の住宅不動産
プロジェクトについて完成後販売の方式を促すほか、未完成物件の予約販売における住宅ローンの実
行時期を物件引き渡し後に変更する。住宅の予約販売制度が不動産ディベロッパーの過度なレバレッ
ジ追求による資金繰り悪化と、それに伴う住宅引き渡し遅延問題を引き起こしたことから、住宅販売
モデルの転換を加速させる。 
住宅都市農村建設部や中国人民銀行、国家金融監督管理総局などの関連省庁が一斉に通達や管理規
定を公布した。住宅販売制度に関する通達では、①予約販売の代金（購入者が支払う頭金と銀行から
振り込まれる住宅ローン）について住宅不動産プロジェクトごとの専用口座を通じた銀行側による資
金管理を強化すること、②新たに不動産用地や建設許可を取得するプロジェクトに対して「完成物件
での販売を優先的に選択」させ、建設許可を取得済みの新規プロジェクトについても完成物件での販
売を奨励すること、③住宅不動産プロジェクトごとに会社を設立し、メインバンクを選定して開発資
金の融資を受けること、などの方針を示した。一方、住宅ローンの新たな管理規定には、予約販売に
おける住宅ローンについて「厳格にプロジェクト竣工届出後に実行しなければならない」と明記した
ほか、これまで最長30年とされてきた住宅ローンの借入期間を40年に変更することを盛り込んだ。 
■ 「保交房」に一定のメド。ディベロッパーの法的整理が進む可能性も 
中国の不動産市場はこれまで、ディベロッパーが物件の竣工を待たずに販売を開始する予約販売方
式が一般的であり、住宅ローンも購入者が予約購入した時点で実行され、返済義務が生じる慣行とな
っていた。予約販売には、購入者にとって不動産価格の値上がり前に物件を確保できるメリットが、
ディベロッパーにとって不動産開発に投じた資金を早期に回収できるメリットがあった。 
しかし、財務レバレッジの追求による収益の最大化を狙うディベロッパーは、回収した予約販売代
金を新たな不動産開発用地の獲得や不動産とは関係のないビジネスへの投資に流用し、事業を急拡大
させた。その実態は自転車操業であり、市場の過熱を懸念した中国政府が2021年にディベロッパーへ
の資金調達規制 （いわゆる 「3つのレッドライン」 ） を正式導入するとたちまち資金繰りに行き詰まり、
社債の債務不履行（デフォルト）が頻発する。ディベロッパーの信用力が低下したところにゼロコロ
2026 年 9 月 30 日

### Page 2

2

ナ政策の強化が追い打ちをかける形で販売が急減し、ディベロッパーの資金繰りが一段と悪化したこ
とで建設工事の進捗が滞り、2022年に入って予約販売済み物件の引き渡し遅延が深刻化する。 
ここに至って予約販売のデメリットが明らかとなる。すなわち、物件引き渡しリスクを負担するの
は購入者であり、たとえ遅延しても住宅ローンの返済を続けなければならないことである。事態を重
く見た中国政府は、「保交楼（予約販売済み未完成物件の引き渡し保証。「保交房」も同じ）」のス
ローガンを掲げ、ディベロッパーの資金繰り支援に乗り出す。2024年1月には、当局が融資適格と判断
した不動産開発プロジェクトをまとめたホワイトリストを作成し、各銀行がこのリストに基づいてデ
ィベロッパーに資金を融通するスキームを構築して、問題の解消を急がせたのである。 
中国政府がこのタイミングで新規則を発表した背景には、この「保交房」に一定のメドが付いたと
判断したことがあるとみられる。事実、国務院の李強首相は2026年3月の全人代における政府活動報告
で「『保交房』の任務は全面的に完成した」と宣言している。また、見逃せない動きとして、広東省
広州市の中級人民法院（地方裁判所）が8月21日、実質的に経営破綻している某ディベロッパーについ
て、債権者による清算の申し立てを受理したことが挙げられる。 
デフォルトを起こした不動産ディベロッパーの多くはこれまで、倒産によって債務の返済や予約販
売物件の引き渡し、建設業者への代金支払いなどの義務・責任を放棄することが許されず、債権者か
ら債務の減免や返済猶予の合意を得つつゾンビ化した状態で経営を続けてきた。ディベロッパーの破
綻が相次げば、不良債権問題の深刻化によって金融システムが動揺するリスクを招くだけでなく、予
約販売物件の引き渡しが困難となって社会問題に発展したり、下請け業者の連鎖倒産で景気が急激に
冷え込む可能性もあったからである。 
今回の動きは、清算の申し立てを裁判所が「受理」しただけではあり、実際に清算が進むのかはな
お予断を許さないが、中国政府がディベロッパーの法的整理を前に進める意思を示したとみることも
できる。今後、清算手続きが順調に進むのか、他のディベロッパーについても同様の動きが広がるの
かを注意深く見守る必要があろう。 
■ 住宅販売モデル転換の社会的意義は大きいが、不動産市況への影響は軽微 
では、住宅販売モデルの転換は不動産市場にどのような影響を与えるであろうか。その前にまず、
不動産市場の現状を整理しておきたい。 
資金調達規制の強化をきっかけとした不動産不況は6年目に突入した。中国の不動産市場規模（販売
金額）は2021～25年にかけて半分に縮小し、販売面積や開発投資額は4割、新規着工面積に至っては7
割も減少した（図表1）。2026年も不況脱却の兆しは見えず、1～8月の販売面積は前年比▲12.1％、開
発投資額は同▲19.9％と低迷が続いている。開発投資の先行指標とされる不動産開発向け銀行貸出も
同▲33.3％となっており、ディベロッパーにとって資金繰りが厳しい状況は変わっていない。 
販売が大きく落ち込んだことで、市場には過剰な在庫が積み上がっている。みずほ総合研究所調査
部の試算によれば、建設中の物件を含む中国の住宅在庫（商用およびオフィス用を除く）は2025年末
時点で40.8億平米に達する（図表2）。2025年の住宅販売面積が7.3億平米であったことから、新規着工
がゼロになると仮定しても在庫をすべて消化するのに5年以上かかる計算である。中国政府は2024年5
月、地方政府による住宅在庫の買い取り策を発表し、同年10月には地方政府が専項債（特別債）の発
行で得た資金を買い取りに使うことも容認したが、在庫の消化は遅々として進んでいない。

### Page 3

3

住宅価格（主要70都市平均）は2021年7月のピーク時から新築で▲14％、中古で▲23％下落し、全体
として下落傾向に歯止めがかかっていない。ただ、図表3のとおり、底堅い需要のある大都市（一級都
市：北京、上海、広州、深圳）と膨大な在庫を抱える地方都市（二級都市：各省の省都。三級都市：
省都以下の主要都市）で二極化が顕著になっている。特に上海は堅調で、その新築住宅価格は2021年
7月から＋24％も上昇している。とはいえ、その堅調さは資産としての不動産保有の魅力を有する一部
の都市に限ったものであり、市況の回復が地方都市にまで広く波及する様子はうかがえない。 
住宅引き渡し遅延問題の発生以降、引き渡しリスクのない完成物件の購入が増加しつつあるが、予
約販売は2025年時点でも依然として住宅販売面積全体の6割超を占めている（図表4）。住宅販売モデ
ルの転換、すなわち予約販売から完成後販売への移行と予約販売における住宅ローン実行の後ろ倒し
は、住宅購入者の物件引き渡しリスクを大幅に軽減することになり、消費者保護の観点からもその社
会的意義は非常に大きいといえる。しかし、これに伴ってディベロッパーの資金繰りはより厳しいも
のとなる。ディベロッパーの資金調達に占める販売代金（頭金などの前受金と住宅ローン）の比率は
4割を超えているからである（図表5）。このため、短期的には新規の開発（土地の取得や新規着工）
図表1 不動産指標（年次） 図表2 住宅在庫

### Page 4

4

が減少する可能性が高い。ただ、新規則は完成
後販売を全国一律に「強制」するのではなく、
あくまで「優先的に選択」させるとしているこ
とから、地方によってはディベロッパーの資金
繰りにも配慮した柔軟な対応がとられる可能
性もある。 
新規制が不動産市況に与える影響は、住宅ロ
ーンの最長借入期間の延長が新たな需要を掘
り起こすとしても、軽微であろう。不動産不況
を脱するためには、過剰在庫の消化とディベロ
ッパーの法的整理、不良債権の処理を着実に進
め、市場を健全な状態に戻すことが不可欠とい
えるが、それにはまだ相当の時間を要するもの
とみられる。

美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，美國財政部
中國預計將減少美元儲備

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

Update
Hiwin Technologies Corp.  | Asia Pacific
September Sales Flat MoM/
+30% YoY
In this report, we focus on Hiwin's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$2,720mn (flat MoM/+30% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue came in at NT$8,110mn (+8% QoQ/+35% YoY), largely in line
with our estimate at NT$7,996mn (+6% QoQ/+33% YoY) and the  consensus
of NT$7,964mn (+6% QoQ/+33% YoY).
Our view:
• On top of the sequential growth on the top line, we believe that Hiwin

should also further expand its gross margin on a QoQ basis in 3Q26, owing
to the higher utilization and the tailwinds of price hikes.
• We think the momentum should remain favorable in the near term.
• We stay constructive on the stock, with current valuation at 29x 2027e P/E
vs. a peak cycle level of 35-40x.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple
of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.

n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 158: AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 10 月 6 日

1/6 
AI がサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株
は上がるのか 
～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ AI によるサイバー攻撃の自動化・高度化が現実のものとなっている。豪政府システムへのAI
侵入事件などが示すように、AI は与えられた目的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超え
てシステムにアクセスしうることが明らかになった。国内でも 9 月下旬以降、幅広い業種の
企業で不正アクセスの公表が相次いでいる。 
○ AI エージェントのサイバー課題解決能力はここ数年で急速に向上しており、サイバーセキュ
リティ企業の売上高・利益も足元で好調に推移している。 
○ 一方で、高度な AI モデルの登場は既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の陳腐化リスクへの懸
念を生み、関連株が下落するというパラドックスも起きている。「サイバー株なら何でも上
がる」という安易な連想買いには注意が必要である。 
○ 個人投資家は、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うのではなく、「AIネイティ
ブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業や、AIを活用した防衛技術を持つ巨大テック企業（メガテ
ック）への選別投資が求められる。

1. 豪政府システムへの「AI侵入事件」が鳴らす警鐘 
2026 年9月、オーストラリアのアルバニージー首相は、同国の公的医療保険制度（Medicare）
の統計ポータルサイトに、OpenAIのAIエージェントが無断で侵入していたと発表した。注目すべ
きは、これが悪意あるハッカーによるサイバー攻撃ではなく、OpenAIの研究チームがAIに調査を
実行させる過程で起きた点だ。AIは調査中にアクセス制限に直面すると、自ら制限を回避する方
法を見つけ出し、非公開のファイルにまでアクセスしたという。この事例は、AIが与えられた目
的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超えてシステムにアクセスしうることを示した。 
サイバー攻撃の脅威は、海の向こうだけの話ではない。日本でも9月下旬以降、物流、交通、
小売、カーシェアリングなど幅広い業種の企業が、不正アクセスによる個人情報流出の可能性を
相次いで公表している。これらの攻撃にAIが用いられたかどうかは明らかになっていないが、サ
イバー攻撃の影響が業種を問わず及んでいることは確かだ。政府も10月1 日から、能動的サイバ

### Page 2

2/6 
ー防御の一環として、重大なサイバー攻撃を未然に防ぐため、警察や自衛隊が攻撃元のサーバー
等にアクセスし、無害化措置を講じることを可能にした。 
AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化・高度化させる時代が到来しつつある。企業や政府の防御需要は急
増し、サイバーセキュリティ企業には特需がもたらされると期待される。個人投資家の間の「AI
の次なる恩恵銘柄はサイバー株」という見方にも、一定の説得力がある。しかし、 「サイバー株な
ら何でも上がる」と考えるのは早計である。AIによる攻撃の自動化・高度化は、攻撃件数を増や
すだけでなく、従来のセキュリティ製品や防御手法の有効性そのものを揺るがしかねないから
だ。本稿では、AIの攻撃能力の現在地と、それがサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績および株価に
与える影響を、データに基づいて検証する。そのうえで、 「サイバー株＝安全確実な成長株」とい
う見方をどう見直すべきか、個人投資家に向けて投資戦略を考えるための新たな視点を提示した
い。

2. AIの攻撃能力急伸とサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績・株価への影響 
(1) AIは人間なしでどこまで攻撃できるのか 
AIエージェントのサイバー攻撃関連能力は急速に高まっている。スタンフォード大学人間中心
AI研究所（Stanford HAI）が公表した「AI Index Report 2026」によれば、AIは人間が逐次指示
を与えなくても、専門的なサイバー課題を自律的に解ける水準に達しつつある。 
同レポートで紹介されている「Cybench」は、暗号、Webセキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリ
ング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど、40の専門レベルのサイバーセキュリティ課題を評
価するベンチマークである。資料1 は、人間のガイドなし（unguided）でAIが課題を解決できた
割合の推移を示している。これを見ると、2024年時点ではわずか15％であった解決率が、2025年
には 82％へと急伸し、2026年には93％に達している。2年間で 78ポイントの上昇であり、AIが
自律的にサイバー課題を解く能力が大きく向上していることがわかる。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 AI エージェントの自律的なサイバー課題解決能力は急伸している 
（注）サイバーセキュリティのベンチマーク「Cybench」における、人間のガイドなし（unguided）での課題解決率（％）の推移。
Cybench は暗号、Web セキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど 40 の専門レベル課題を評価
するもの。 
（出所）Stanford HAI, “AI Index Report 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) サイバー需要は企業の売上・利益に届いているのか 
こうしたAIによるサイバー攻撃の脅威が高まるなか、防御側であるサイバーセキュリティ企業
への需要拡大が期待される。実際、その需要はすでに企業業績に表れている。 
Nasdaqが公表した「Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary」によれば、サイバーセキュリ
ティ関連銘柄で構成される「Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL） 」構成企業の
2026 年第1 四半期決算は極めて好調であった。同レポートによると、構成企業の売上高は前年同
期比で 11％増加し、純利益は同29.3％の増加を記録しており、売上高以上に純利益が大きく伸び
ていることが確認できる。さらに、市場予想に対する結果をまとめた資料2を見ても、売上高で
予想を上回った企業は指数ウェイトの92.2％を占め、純利益に至っては100％の企業が予想を上
回った。サイバーセキュリティ需要の強さは、売上高と利益の双方に表れている。

### Page 4

4/6 
資料 2 サイバーセキュリティ企業の業績は市場予想を大きく上回る 
（注）Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL）構成企業の 2026 年第 1 四半期決算の集計結果。市場予想に対する売上
高および純利益の予想超え・予想未達の企業数（指数ウェイトベース）、ならびに平均上振れ・下振れ率（％）を示す。 
（出所）Nasdaq, “Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) それでもサイバー株は上がるのか 
業績が好調であれば株価も上昇すると考えるのが自然だが、 「AIによる攻撃高度化＝サイバー株
上昇」とは必ずしも言い切れない。AIは防御需要を増やす一方で、既存のサイバーセキュリティ
製品を陳腐化させるとの懸念も生んでいるからだ。 
国際決済銀行（BIS）が2026 年7月に公表したレポート「A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and 
cyber risk」は、このパラドックスを指摘している。2026年 4月にAnthropicが高度なサイバー攻
撃能力を持つAIモデル「Mythos」を発表した際、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は大きく下落し
た。資料3 は、Mythos発表前を0とした場合のサイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン
（CAR）を示している。発表後、サイバーセキュリティETF（IHAK）や主要企業（CRWD、PANWな
ど）のCARは軒並みマイナスに転じ、一部の系列ではグラフ上で約−30％の大幅なマイナスを記録
した。BISは、この市場反応について「新しいAIモデルの登場により、既存のサイバーセキュリテ
ィ製品やサービスが陳腐化する（obsolescence）という投資家の懸念を反映している」との解釈
を紹介している。つまり、AIの進化はサイバーセキュリティ市場全体を拡大させる可能性が高い
ものの、同時にゲームチェンジを引き起こし、旧来の技術やサービスに依存する企業の競争力を
急速に奪うリスクを孕んでいる。

### Page 5

5/6 
資料 3 高度な AI モデルの発表後、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は下落した 
（注）2026 年 4 月の高度な AI モデル「Mythos」発表前後の、サイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン（CAR、％）。発表前
を 0 として推移を示している。「Glasswing partners」は CrowdStrike（CRWD）および Palo Alto Networks（PANW）を指す。 
（出所）Bank for International Settlements, “BIS Bulletin No. 129: A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and cyber risk”をもとに第一ラ
イフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. AI時代のサイバー株投資～「陳腐化リスク」を回避する新戦略～ 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI はサイバー攻撃能力を飛躍的に高めている。また、足元のサイバ
ーセキュリティ企業の業績も好調である。しかし、最先端のAIモデルが登場した際には、市場は
既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の「陳腐化リスク」に敏感に反応し、関連株は大きく売り込ま
れた。ここからいえるのは、サイバーセキュリティ市場全体は今後も拡大が見込まれるものの、
企業間の「勝ち負け」はこれまで以上に鮮明になるということだ。こうした環境変化のなか、個
人投資家には従来の投資アプローチを根本から見直すことが求められる。 
第一に、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うアプローチ（テーマ型ETFやインデ
ックスファンドなど）には注意が必要である。市場全体が成長しても、AIの進化に取り残され、
陳腐化して淘汰される旧世代の企業をポートフォリオに抱え込むリスクが高まるからだ。足元の
業績や過去の市場シェアだけで安心することはできない。第二に、個別銘柄を選別する際は、 「AI
ネイティブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業であるかどうかが最大の焦点となる。攻撃側がAI を駆
使する以上、防御側もAIをシステムの中心に据え、未知の脅威を自律的に検知・遮断できるプラ
ットフォームを持っていなければ対抗できない。レガシーな製品を販売している企業ではなく、
クラウドベースで膨大なデータをAI で解析し、防御機能を継続的に高度化できる企業を見極める
必要がある。第三に、サイバー防衛の投資対象を「セキュリティ専業企業」以外にも広げること
である。 
最先端のAI モデルを開発し、強固なクラウドインフラを持つ巨大テック企業（メガテック）
は、自社のAI を活用した防衛技術を、顧客向けの強力なセキュリティサービスとして提供し始め

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

ている。豊富な資金力とAI 開発力を持つメガテックを、サイバー防衛の担い手として評価し、ポ
ートフォリオの中核に据えることも、AI時代の投資戦略として有効な選択肢となるだろう。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the
annual pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. The result was broadly
in line with Westpac’s expectation for a 5.0%mth decline,
although notably weaker than the market expectation for a
1.0%mth fall. July’s decline in private detached dwellings

proved short lived, with approvals rebounding in August.
Instead, weakness in the headline result was driven by a
sizeable decline in private unit approvals.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals rose 3.7%mth in August,
following a large 2.5ppt upward revision to July’s 1.9%mth
decline. The increase was the strongest in more than a year,

with private detached dwelling approvals reaching their highest
level since August 2021. With the annual pace strengthening
to 18.4%yr, detached dwelling approvals continue to appear
resilient despite recent interest rate increases. Some of this
resilience may partly reflect the implementation of changes to
SMSF property borrowing arrangements on 10 August.
Private unit approvals drove the decline in the headline result,
falling 21.2%mth in August. Following a 1.2ppt upward

revision to July’s 0.8%mth increase, the annual pace returned to
contractionary territory at –2.1%yr. Weakness was broad-based
across segments, with both high-rise and low-mid-rise
approvals declining in the month.
Queensland recorded the weakest result, with private approvals
falling 22.6%mth, driven primarily by weakness in unit approvals,
while private approvals in NSW also declined, falling 17.1%mth.
By contrast, WA (+0.2%mth), Victoria (+8.4%mth) and South

Australia (+32.0%mth) all recorded gains over the month.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 0.6%mth,
driven by a 0.5%mth decline in new residential approvals
and a 2.1%mth fall in renovation activity. Non-residential
approvals declined sharply, down 44.8%mth. Volatility
continues to be driven by the data centre-dominated ‘other
commercial’ segment, which fell 91.1%mth on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. Excluding this segment, in non-seasonally

adjusted terms, non-residential approvals fell a more subdued
13.9%mth (from -37.9%mth). The total value of building
approvals declined 21.3%mth in August.
August’s result suggests dwelling approvals continue to
moderate following the strength recorded earlier in the year.
However, the underlying picture remains more resilient, with
the headline decline driven by volatility in unit approvals
while detached dwellings strengthened. NSW and Victoria,

traditionally among the more interest rate sensitive housing
markets, recorded the weakest detached dwelling outcomes,
potentially signalling broader rate effects ahead. For a
comprehensive assessment of housing market conditions, see
our latest Housing Pulse (see here).
Detached approvals point to some resilience

Document 234: Energy Bills_ Short-term relief, longer-term challenges Utility bill inflation has moderated, but household payments remain elevated and longer-term pressures are building. 2 min r

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy Bills_ Short-term relief, longer-term challenges Utility bill inflation has moderated, but household payments remain elevated and longer-term pressures are building. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01

### Page 1

13018305     1

Economy 
Energy bills: Short-term relief, longer-term challenges 
16 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Energy bill inflation was around 4% year-over-year (YoY) in August 2026. However, Bank of America internal deposit data shows 
average utility bill payments rose faster, increasing 5% YoY from June through August. This was likely due, in part, to higher 
cooling demand during a warm summer. 
• But bill pressures remain highly uneven across the country. Average utility payments have risen by more than 10% YoY in cities 
such as Detroit, Baltimore and Washington DC, while consumers in Orlando, San Jose and Tampa have seen declines. Some of 
these regional differences appear to reflect policy and infrastructure factors as much as energy usage itself. 
• The long-term outlook still points to upward pressure on electricity bills as demand from data center construction, electrification 
and broader economic activity drive the need for additional generation and grid investment. However, a forecasted strong El 
Niño event this winter could provide some near-term relief, if warmer-than-normal temperatures reduce household heating 
demand, partially offsetting structural cost pressures.

Energy bill inflation has moderated  
Energy is an essential household expense. According to Bureau of Labor Statistics data, energy bills accounted for roughly 3% of 
total household spending as of 2024. US utility prices have been far less affected by higher global oil prices stemming from the 
conflict in the Middle East than gasoline prices, largely because the US is a net exporter of natural gas. Nonetheless, the cost of 
energy has been rising for a prolonged period: since January 2024 the price of electricity has risen 10% and the price of piped 
gas is up 18%.  
The better news is that utility price inflation has moderated relative to the second half of 2025 and early 2026. As of August, 
electricity and piped gas prices were up around 4% year-over-year (YoY). But Bank of America internal deposit data shows that 
average utility payments for electricity and gas were running ahead of inflation in these categories, up 5.3% YoY. One reason 
could be that household demand for cooling was strong this summer, due to relatively warm weather (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: Utility bill payments rose around 5.3% YoY in August

deposit data (% YoY)

Energy costs vary widely by region 
While energy bills account for a relatively modest share of household spending nationally, experiences vary across the country. 
Looking at large cities (by Core Based Statistical Areas), our data shows that utility bills rose by more than 10% YoY in Detroit, 
Baltimore and Washington DC from June through August 2026. At the other end of the spectrum, bills fell YoY in Orlando, San 
Joe and Tampa (Exhibit 2).  
Customers’ bills are being pressured by reshoring, reindustrialization, the general pass-through costs in the economy, and a 
significant need for infrastructure replacement in many markets. Regulatory changes and one-off charges have also influenced 
costs in several areas. In Florida, for example, the removal of hurricane recovery surcharges reduced utility bills in 2026, while in 
the Northeast, investments in grid infrastructure and capacity have contributed to higher costs.  
There also remains some disparities in where average bills sit relative to the overall US (Exhibit 3). In Washington DC, Boston, 
Phoenix and Dallas, the average utility bill payment in June through August 2026 was well above the US average. Conversely, 
Chicago, Nashville and Las Vegas paid some of the lowest energy bills relative to the US.

Rising demand for electricity will continue to pressure bills  
Looking longer-term, rising energy demand (particularly for electricity), driven by factors such as data center expansion and 
residential electrification, is likely to keep upward pressure on consumers’ energy bills for a number of years (read Is the AI 
revolution raising consumers' utility bills? and Can the AI buildout keep building? for more).  
Exhibit 4 shows forecasts from the US Energy Information Administration’s August 2026 Short-term Energy Outlook, estimating 
a 4% YoY rise in commercial and industrial electricity consumption. Accommodating this growth will likely require ongoing 
investment in grid capacity and power generation, and some of these costs may be passed on to consumers through higher 
energy bills.  
Exhibit 4: The demand for electricity is rising particularly from commercial and industrial sectors 
US electrical demand (in billion kilowatt hours) 2020-2027E (estimates/forecast from 2026)

Source: US Energy Information Administration 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Short-term, the weather could prove important  
El Niño is the warming phase of ENSO (El Niño–Southern Oscillation), a global climate phenomenon that emerges from variation 
in winds and sea surface temperatures over the Pacific Ocean. El Niño events can have widespread and profound global impacts, 
causing extreme weather such as storms, floods and higher temperatures. The strongest recorded El Niño took place in 1877 to 
1878 and led to a global famine that killed more than 50 million people.  
The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) believes there is a high probability that a very strong El Niño 
event will occur this year (Exhibit 5). One way to put this in historical context is through the NOAA’s Relative Oceanic Niño Index 
(RONI). A positive RONI indicates the central/eastern tropical Pacific is unusually warm relative to the rest of the tropics. A 
higher RONI indicates a more severe El Niño, though NOAA points out that uncertainty on impacts also tends to rise with RONI.  
Exhibit 5: The NOAA attaches a very high probability to a severe El Niño event over coming months  
Likelihood of an El Nino event estimated by severity (%)

Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA)

Exhibit 6 shows that the current forecast for RONI from October to December 2026 is one of the largest on record. In recent 
history, similar-sized El Niño events have only occurred in 1997-98, 2015-16 and to a lesser extent in 2022-23.

For the US, significant El Niño events tend to bring higher-than-usual temperatures and more rainfall (Exhibit 7). However, no El 
Niño is exactly the same. For example, in 1997-98 California was flooded in but in 2015-16 it was dry, while the nation overall 
was wet. Both time periods were among the highest levels of RONI in the past 70 years.

I NSTI TUTE

What does this mean for consumers and their energy bills? The most predictable impact from a severe El Niño appears to be 
unseasonably warm temperatures, implying reduced heating needs during the fall and winter. Consumers could therefore receive 
some relief in their utility bills and potentially have additional income to spend on discretionary goods and services over the 
coming months.  
There is a large caveat, however. If El Niño manifests in stormy and rainy conditions – likely impacting the southern parts of the 
US – then some households may end up having to spend on repairs and maintenance following weather damage (read more on 
consumer spending in the aftermath of natural disasters in October’s What's the impact of natural disasters on local 
economies?). This might boost consumer discretionary spending, but not in a way that implies improved well-being. And longer 
term, the broader impact of both climate change and energy demands from the AI revolution are likely to keep upward pressure 
on bills.

### Page 6

6 16 September 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Poland’s September inﬂation was revised up to 4.1% YoY, with

inﬂation accelerated to 3.6% YoY and services prices slowed to
5.3% YoY, compared with 2.5% YoY and 5.6% YoY respectively in
August. Services inﬂation has fallen to its lowest level in ﬁve
months, with no evidence of second-round eﬀects.

Price growth continues to be driven primarily by rising energy costs.
Compared with August, fuel prices increased by 9.2% MoM, while
household energy prices rose by 1.0% MoM, mainly due to higher
prices for liquid fuels (+14.7% MoM) and solid fuels (+5.5% MoM),
including coal (+7.4% MoM). Higher energy prices contributed
around 2.5 percentage points, or roughly 60% of September's
annual inﬂation rate.
Food prices were broadly unchanged (+0.1% MoM), as were prices in

core categories. We estimate that core inﬂation excluding food and
energy slowed to around 3.1% YoY from 3.3% YoY in August. The
deceleration in services inﬂation suggests that the energy shock is
not spreading into other price categories. In September, even
categories linked to the AI investment boom were not very
buoyant.

In our view, if oil prices remain elevated, the ﬁscal programme is

generated by the start of the war in 2026, and smooth headline
inﬂation.
An external supply shock with limited
broader price pressures
At present, higher inﬂation is primarily the result of an external
supply shock in the form of rising energy prices, while prices in
other categories remain relatively stable. Food remains cheap, and

there are no clear signs of broader inﬂationary pressure or
signiﬁcant second-round eﬀects.
However, the prolonged energy crisis has somewhat worsened the
inﬂation outlook. The beginning of next year will probably bring
substantial increases in regulated household prices, particularly
electricity and gas tariﬀs. Food prices are also likely to rise,
reﬂecting higher agricultural commodity prices, a low comparison
base and more expensive fertilisers.

The MPC can afford to wait, but rate hikes
in 1Q27 cannot be ruled out
We believe that, under current conditions, the Monetary Policy
Council does not need to rush into tightening monetary policy. The
November inﬂation projection may initiate a discussion about
whether interest rate increases are warranted.
In our view, rate hikes could take place in 1Q27, as further months
of elevated energy prices increase the risk that the energy shock

Document 250: France isn’t the new Greece, but the warning signs are familiar

Source PDF: all report pdf/France isn’t the new Greece, but the warning signs are familiar.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

France's political and ﬁscal challenges have sparked
comparisons with the eurozone debt crisis. But while some
warning signs look familiar, key diﬀerences mean today's
France is not Greece in 2010 and fears of a new euro crisis are
overblown
Carsten Brzeski
History hardly ever repeats. It rhymes. More than 16 years after the
eurozone sovereign debt crisis, France's political and ﬁscal troubles
have revived memories of that period. However, as is so often the

case with memories, particularly those of experts and market
participants who weren’t even around at that time, they tend to be
blurry and oversimpliﬁed. Luckily, yours truly and other members
of ING’s Research team were around at that time and can call
themselves true euro crisis veterans.
A trip back to the euro crisis
Let me take you on a trip down memory lane. The euro crisis began
with a revision, not a recession. In October 2009, the newly elected

PASOK government of George Papandreou announced that the
2009 budget deﬁcit would be around 12.7% of GDP, roughly double
what Athens had previously reported. Eurostat later put the ﬁnal
ﬁgure at 15.4%. Overnight, the problem was no longer the size of
the hole; it was that nobody had known how deep it was. Suspicion
that Greece had fought its way into eurozone membership with
creative accounting accompanied the entire euro crisis.
After the statistical revelation, the numbers were brutal: public debt

was close to 130% of GDP and heading for 150%. The current
account deﬁcit had hit around 15% of GDP in 2008: Greece was
borrowing abroad not only to fund its state but its entire economy.
Unit labour costs in Greece had risen much faster than in Germany
Cookie settings
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
Here you can specify which type of cookies you consent to and
which you prefer to disable. Once you've made your selection,

please conﬁrm your preferences. You can change your cookie
preferences here at any time.
Read more about our Cookie policy
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies
Conﬁrm selection Accept all

### Page 2

since euro entry. Interestingly, markets did not panic at once. When
the revision came out, the 10-year Greek spread over Bunds was
around 130bp, about where France trades now. It took six months
of failed promises, rating downgrades and EU dithering to turn that
into a funding crisis.
Shortly before Greece ﬁnally received a European bailout, and the
infamous troika of the European Commission, the ECB and IMF
basically took over policymaking in Greece, the spread between

Greek and German debt was above 900bp. And actually it was
above 3000bp two years later, at the time of the second bailout
and haircuts on Greek debt.
It didn’t take long before the debt crisis spread from Greece to other
European capitals. Not at once but over two years, with spreads vis-
à-vis Germany reaching around 1500bp in the case of Portugal,
1100bp in the case of Ireland and around 600bp for Spain and Italy.
The transmission channels of contagion diﬀered. While the bailout

of its banks blew up the Irish budget deﬁcit to 32% of GDP, Spain
saw its real estate market imploding with regional banks going
bust, Portugal suﬀered from an enormous current account deﬁcit
and high private debt and, last but not least, Italy got into trouble
due to high government debt, low growth and a lack of political
credibility. What these countries had in common was a lack of
market conﬁdence in the existence of a credible lender of last resort

and the unresolved doom loop between banks and sovereigns.
It took Mario Draghi’s ‘whatever it takes’, a multi-layered ﬁrewall of
new European (rescue) institutions, as well as the political will in
European capitals to implement austerity measures and structural
reforms, to stop the crisis.
Why France is not Greece
We receive many questions about whether France is the new Greece
and whether we are stumbling into the next euro debt crisis. And,
indeed, at least at ﬁrst glance, some numbers are worrying:

France's debt ratio is now similar to that of Italy in 2011 and Greece
in 2009. The French ﬁscal deﬁcit is wider than Italy's was then. The
primary deﬁcit – the gap before interest – is close to 3% of GDP.

Document 267: How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales

### Page 1

Agentic commerce, where autonomous AI agents
independently select and purchase products on behalf of
consumers, is forcing retailers to rethink their role. As AI takes
over more of the buying process, physical stores are evolving
from a sales channel into a destination for advice, service,
experience, and omnichannel support
Katinka Jongkind
A Galaxy General AI robot retail store, Shanghai. Retail is entering a new phase
of digital disruption, where agentic commerce could fundamentally change

how consumers shop
Agentic commerce fundamentally changes
the buying process
Retail is entering a new phase of digital disruption. E-commerce
has already transformed where consumers shop; agentic
commerce is set to change how they shop. Autonomous artiﬁcial
intelligence agents can search, compare and purchase products on
behalf of consumers, shifting shopping from an active task to an
increasingly automated background process.

### Page 2

Agentic commerce goes beyond AI
assistants and comparison websites
Key diﬀerences between agentic commerce, AI assistants (such as
ChatGPT and Gemini) and comparison websites
Source: ING Research
Most relevant for routine purchases, less so
for luxury goods
Agentic commerce is not equally suited to every product category.
Its greatest potential lies in repeat and replacement purchases, as
well as functional, standardised products. In these categories, AI

agents can eﬃciently compare prices, speciﬁcations, and
availability, while using consumption patterns to determine the
optimal time to buy.
Adoption is likely to be slower for luxury, trend-sensitive and
experience-driven products. In these categories, inspiration,
emotion, and personal preference remain central to the purchasing
decision, making consumers less inclined to relinquish control. AI
agents can assist by pre-selecting products that match predeﬁned

preferences, but ﬁnal purchasing decisions are likely to remain
ﬁrmly in the hands of the consumer.

### Page 3

Consumer electronics and personal care
lead the adoption of agentic commerce
Within non-food retail, agentic commerce appears particularly well
suited to consumer electronics and personal care. Both sectors are
characterised by a high degree of product standardisation and a
large share of repeat purchases, making them ideal candidates for
AI-driven purchasing.
In consumer electronics, purchasing decisions are often based on
objective and easily comparable criteria, including price, technical

speciﬁcations, and availability. This makes products such as
televisions, laptops and other electronic devices well suited to
automation, allowing AI agents to manage much of the purchasing
journey from product selection to the ﬁnal transaction.
The potential is equally signiﬁcant in personal care, where many
purchases are routine and repetitive. Products such as diapers,
shampoo, and toothpaste can be reordered automatically based
on usage patterns and preset preferences. By reducing the eﬀort

required to order everyday products, AI agents could further
accelerate the shift towards online shopping.
Agentic commerce is best suited to
consumer electronics and personal care
products
Product categories most and least suited to agentic commerce

Source: ING Research
Personal taste limits AI agents' potential in
fashion and living
For now, fashion and home furnishings appear less suited to fully
autonomous agentic commerce. Purchasing decisions in these
categories are often shaped by inspiration, aesthetics, personal
taste, and broader lifestyle considerations, making consumers less
willing to hand over control to an AI agent.
While AI can support product discovery and narrow down options

based on predeﬁned preferences, the ﬁnal purchase decision will
typically remain with the consumer. In these segments, AI is
therefore more likely to serve as a decision-support tool than as a
fully autonomous purchasing agent.
Chinese platforms lead adoption
Agentic commerce creates the greatest opportunities for large
global marketplaces and technology companies. These players
combine vast product assortments with the scale, data and
infrastructure needed to deploy AI agents eﬀectively. Their

integrated ecosystems, including payment solutions, logistics
networks, and data platforms, enable them to integrate AI agents
into their business operations faster and more eﬀectively.
Chinese platforms such as Alibaba and JD.com currently lead the
way, with AI agents already deeply integrated into their respective
platform networks. These agents can not only provide product

recommendations but also complete purchases autonomously.
Meanwhile, US technology companies including Amazon, Google,
Meta, OpenAI and Anthropic are investing heavily in the
development and rollout of AI agents, intensifying the global race
for leadership in this emerging market.
European players are structurally lagging behind their American
and Chinese competitors. Stricter regulation and less integrated

services. While Zalando is among Europe’s frontrunners in AI-
powered retail, its applications are currently focused on product
discovery, comparison, and selection. Fully autonomous
purchasing has yet to become a reality.
Technology platforms are becoming the
gatekeepers of commerce
AI agents are rarely neutral actors. Embedded within commercial
ecosystems, they are inherently linked to the interests of platforms,
marketplaces and technology companies. Although agentic

commerce promises greater convenience and time savings for
consumers, it is also likely to strengthen the dominance of large
tech ﬁrms and marketplaces. These parties are becoming
increasingly inﬂuential in shaping product choices and controlling
consumer access.
Physical stores are not disappearing, but
their role is changing
How will the role of the physical store evolve in an AI-driven retail
landscape?

Redeﬁning the store in the age of AI
Agentic commerce is expected to accelerate the shift of sales to
online channels, putting further pressure on the existence of
physical stores. The extent of this impact will largely depend on
consumers’ willingness to delegate purchasing decisions to AI
agents.
Physical stores will nevertheless remain relevant for many non-food
retailers. Their role is shifting from sales channel to destination for

advice, service, inspiration, and support of online sales. Stores that
continue to rely mainly on transactional sales risk losing relevance
in an increasingly AI-driven retail landscape.
Retailers that successfully integrate their physical and digital
channels, deﬁne clear roles for their stores, and deploy technology
strategically can strengthen their position within the customer
journey. In a future shaped by AI-driven commerce, their role will

increasingly centre on adding value where automation cannot.

Document 273: ING Monthly_ Same fears, different crisis

Source PDF: all report pdf/ING Monthly_ Same fears, different crisis.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 41
Status: ok

### Page 1

Monthly Economic Update October 2026

14 October 2026

www.ing.com/THINK

ING Monthly 
October 2026

THINK Economic and Financial Analysis

Same fears, different crisis

### Page 2

Monthly Economic Update October 2026

2 
Same fears, different crisis 
France's political and fiscal challenges have sparked comparisons with the eurozone 
debt crisis. But while some warning signs look familiar, key differences mean today's 
France is not Greece in 2010 and fears of a new euro crisis are overblown 
History hardly ever repeats. It rhymes. More than 16 years after the eurozone sovereign 
debt crisis, France's political and fiscal troubles have revived memories of that period. 
However, as is so often the case with memories, particularly those of experts and 
market participants who weren’t even around at that time, they tend to be blurry and 
oversimplified. Luckily, yours truly and other members of ING’s Research team were 
around at that time and can call themselves true euro crisis veterans. 
A trip back to the euro crisis 
Let me take you on a trip down memory lane. The euro crisis began with a revision, not 
a recession. In October 2009, the newly elected PASOK government of George 
Papandreou announced that the 2009 budget deficit would be around 12.7% of GDP, 
roughly double what Athens had previously reported. Eurostat later put the final figure 
at 15.4%. Overnight, the problem was no longer the size of the hole; it was that nobody 
had known how deep it was. Suspicion that Greece had fought its way into eurozone 
membership with creative accounting accompanied the entire euro crisis. 
After the statistical revelation, the numbers were brutal: public debt was close to 130% 
of GDP and heading for 150%. The current account deficit had hit around 15% of GDP in 
2008: Greece was borrowing abroad not only to fund its state but its entire economy. 
Unit labour costs in Greece had risen much faster than in Germany since euro entry. 
Interestingly, markets did not panic at once. When the revision came out, the 10-year 
Greek spread over Bunds was around 130bp, about where France trades now. It took six 
months of failed promises, rating downgrades and EU dithering to turn that into a 
funding crisis. 
Shortly before Greece finally received a European bailout, and the infamous troika of the 
European Commission, the ECB and IMF basically took over policymaking in Greece, the 
spread between Greek and German debt was above 900bp. And actually it was above 
3000bp two years later, at the time of the second bailout and haircuts on Greek debt. 
It didn’t take long before the debt crisis spread from Greece to other European capitals. 
Not at once but over two years, with spreads vis-à-vis Germany reaching around 1500bp 
in the case of Portugal, 1100bp in the case of Ireland and around 600bp for Spain and 
Italy. The transmission channels of contagion differed. While the bailout of its banks 
blew up the Irish budget deficit to 32% of GDP, Spain saw its real estate market 
imploding with regional banks going bust, Portugal suffered from an enormous current 
account deficit and high private debt and, last but not least, Italy got into trouble due to 
high government debt, low growth and a lack of political credibility. What these 
countries had in common was a lack of market confidence in the existence of a credible 
lender of last resort and the unresolved doom loop between banks and sovereigns. 
It took Mario Draghi’s ‘whatever it takes’, a multi-layered firewall of new European 
(rescue) institutions, as well as the political will in European capitals to implement 
austerity measures and structural reforms, to stop the crisis.

### Page 3

Monthly Economic Update October 2026

3 
Why France is not Greece 
We receive many questions about whether France is the new Greece and whether we 
are stumbling into the next euro debt crisis. And, indeed, at least at first glance, some 
numbers are worrying: France's debt ratio is now similar to that of Italy in 2011 and 
Greece in 2009. The French fiscal deficit is wider than Italy's was then. The primary 
deficit – the gap before interest – is close to 3% of GDP. Closing it requires a political 
consensus that France has not produced since the 1970s. Most worrying is the French 
track record of missing its own and European targets for years, if not decades. Greece's 
credibility was destroyed by false statistics; France's is being eroded by false promises 
and the inability or unwillingness to start fiscal and structural reforms. 
While these elements are clearly frightening, there are also significant differences 
between France now and Greece back then. While Greece had a substantial current 
account deficit, France’s current account is basically in balance, the household savings 
rate is at around 18% and the effective interest rate France pays on its whole debt stock 
is still below nominal GDP growth. Greece in 2010 paid around 5% on its debt stock while 
the economy was shrinking. France has a fiscal problem but not a funding problem. 
What's more, the eurozone now has a firewall that it didn’t have 16 years ago. 
Admittedly, the firewall has been constructed for smaller eurozone countries, not for 
France or Italy. 
All of this means that fears about a new euro debt crisis are overdone. Fears about the 
political ability and willingness to bring national economies and the entire EU on course 
for reforms and sustainable public finances, unfortunately, are not.

Watch: Debt crisis 2.0? Why France isn't the new Greece

carsten.brzeski@ing.de

### Page 4

Monthly Economic Update October 2026

4 
Our key calls this month 
• Energy prices: Despite growing tensions, we still expect oil prices to fall materially 
into year-end. We’re sticking to our forecast for Brent crude to average $80/bbl 
through the fourth quarter. European gas is the bigger near-term risk; we expect 
Dutch TTF to average €75/MWh this quarter before falling sharply from 2Q27 as 
supply normalises. 
• United States: We expect the Fed to hike rates again in December, but we don’t 
expect further increases in 2027 as inflation pressure begins to fade. 
• Eurozone: Growth has proved resilient but higher energy costs, rising bond yields and 
French fiscal uncertainty should slow growth through winter. We expect one more 
ECB hike in December, taking the deposit rate to 2.75%. 
• China: The recent State Council meeting appeared to signal a more proactive policy 
push amid flagging growth. Should economic momentum remain subdued into year-
end, policymakers may respond with further fiscal stimulus and a broader easing of 
monetary policy. 
• United Kingdom: Inflation looks set to briefly top 4% next year and that's likely to 
trigger a November insurance rate hike from the Bank of England. We tentatively 
expect that to be followed by another hike in February. 
• Market rates: The balance of probabilities suggests there is at least one more leg of 
long-end weakness still to play out. This could take the US 10-year yield back into 
the 5.25% to 5.5% range. For later in 2027, we think inflation will come down, and 
long rates should too. 
• FX: We see limited scope for a near-term EUR/USD rebound. Key factors for EUR/USD 
now are a hawkish Fed and a re-pricing of the ECB cycle on French fiscal concerns. 
Neither of those factors appears set for an imminent reversal, leaving EUR/USD to 
languish in a new 1.10-1.15 range.

### Page 6

Monthly Economic Update October 2026

6

Oil prices are holding firm despite Persian Gulf supply increasing

Persian Gulf oil flows pick-up 
There are few signs of de-escalation in the Middle East. If anything, it appears as though 
the conflict is broadening, with the Houthis in Yemen increasingly targeting Saudi 
Arabia. Despite little improvement in the geopolitical environment, oil flows from the 
Persian Gulf have been trending higher with supply from the region returning to 70-75% 
of pre-war levels, although there are some estimates suggesting we could be back to as 
high as 80-90% of pre-war flows. 
The fact that oil prices are well-supported despite the improvement in flows from the 
region appears to reflect concerns over how sustainable this supply is, particularly when 
considering the geopolitical backdrop. The market continues to price in a large risk 
premium in the absence of a deal between the US and Iran. And to see this risk premium 
erode, the market may need the comfort of a deal. Failing that, time may be enough for 
oil prices to trend lower, assuming supplies from the region continue to hold up. For now, 
we are holding onto our 4Q26 Brent forecast of $80/bbl and FY27 forecast of $71/bbl. 
However, given the high level of uncertainty, this is a low-conviction call, specifically for 
4Q26. 
As oil flows from the Persian Gulf have picked up, another bottleneck has become 
increasingly noticeable in the oil market. There is significant tightness in the tanker 
market, which has seen tanker freight rates surge to record levels. Oil flows from the 
Persian Gulf have become increasingly inefficient due to the challenging environment, 
which has absorbed more of the global tanker fleet. And the only way this starts to 
normalise is if the way oil flows from the region also starts to normalise. 
The other pain point for the oil market remains diesel, which will be a concern as we 
head further into the Northern Hemisphere winter. Disruptions from the Persian Gulf and 
Russia have tightened the market, while the risk of export restrictions from the US has 
kept the market nervous. The risk of US export restrictions appears to have subsided, 
Warren Patterson 
Head of Commodities Strategy 
warren.patterson@ing.com

Oil prices hold firm despite Persian 
Gulf supply growing  
Oil prices remain firm despite rising supply from the Persian Gulf. We expect oil prices to 
trade lower into year-end. More positive signs of a potential US-Iran deal would give us 
more confidence in this view. Meanwhile, European gas looks vulnerable heading deeper 
into the heating season

### Page 7

Monthly Economic Update October 2026

7 
with Europe set to release diesel from strategic reserves. Furthermore, expensive freight 
suggests that refined product prices are likely to remain elevated. 
Natural gas vulnerable heading into heating season 
European natural gas prices have traded at their highest level since December 2022, 
with supply concerns growing as the region heads into the heating season. 
Unfortunately for the natural gas market, we have not seen a similar recovery in LNG 
flows from the Persian Gulf as we have with oil. Therefore, LNG flows from the region 
remain down about 80% YoY. The market is increasingly accepting that Persian Gulf LNG 
could remain largely absent from global markets for much of the Northern Hemisphere 
winter. This means Asia and Europe will continue competing for spot cargoes throughout 
the winter, keeping prices well supported and leaving the market vulnerable to further 
spikes. 
European gas storage remains a concern, with it a little under 75% full, well below the 
five-year average of 89%. Low storage has seen the German government instruct state-
owned SEFE to buy and store an additional 8TWh of natural gas by 15 December. The 
prospect of essentially forced buying provides further support to the market. 
With little sign of an imminent recovery in Persian Gulf LNG supply, weather will be 
crucial this winter, and there is growing expectation that Europe will experience a winter 
of milder temperatures due to El Niño, which would provide some relief to the market. 
However, it is not a strong relationship, and El Niño does not guarantee milder 
temperatures. The North Asia winter will also be important for the LNG market, as El 
Niño can also typically mean milder temperatures in this region. 
We now expect TTF to average EUR73/MWh over the 26/27 winter, up from a previous 
forecast of EUR65/MWh. The market will likely be very volatile over this period, given the 
lower starting point for gas storage. Prices are then expected to sell off aggressively 
from 2Q27 as Persian Gulf supply normalises, and we see a further ramp-up in US LNG 
export capacity.

Our latest views on the major central 
banks 
Our take on what could be next for the Federal Reserve, the European Central Bank, the 
Bank of England and the Bank of Japan over the coming months

### Page 11

Monthly Economic Update October 2026

11 
intervention. For that, the French government would first have to implement austerity 
measures before the ECB would ever consider starting any bond purchases. 
However, beyond the question of restarting bond purchases, the rise in bond yields has 
already impacted eurozone growth and inflation. In fact, the surge in German bond 
yields since the September meeting has already had roughly the same impact on 
growth and inflation as an additional 25bp rate hike. 
For France, it's closer to a 50bp hike. With bond markets doing the ECB's job, some 
central bank officials might be less keen to continue hiking than they were at the 
September meeting. 
Bank of England 
We now expect two rate hikes from the Bank of England – in November and February. 
Until now, the Bank has cast its decision not to cut rates, as implicitly planned before the 
Iran War, as de facto tightening. But recent comments suggest it increasingly feels it 
needs to act. 
The Bank’s November inflation forecasts are likely to show CPI briefly above 4%, and that 
is likely to be the catalyst for a hike, citing previous research that inflation at these levels 
is historically more likely to trigger second-round effects. 
But the case for hikes is still not compelling. With the jobs market still relatively cool, 
we’re sceptical that wage growth is likely to react to higher energy prices anywhere 
near as much as it did four years ago. Interest rates are also more evidently restrictive 
than they are in the eurozone or the US. That still points to rate cuts from late 2027 and 
into 2028. 
Bank of Japan 
The market prices in very low chances of a back-to-back BoJ hike on 30 October 
following its 25bp hike to 1.25% last month. That move saw two dissenters, and the BoJ 
typically takes time to assess the impact of policy changes before further adjustments. 
Nonetheless, the central bank continues to see upside risks to inflation and seems intent 
on a terminal policy rate somewhere in the 1.75-2.00% area next year. 
Most in the market are expecting the next hike to come in December, seeing the BoJ 
switching to a quarterly cycle from a six-monthly cycle of tightening policy. That faster 
pace of tightening would seemingly be in accord with Washington's desire for BoJ 
monetary policy to provide some credible macro support to July's joint US-Japan 
intervention in the yen. 
We see risks that the next hike could be pushed back into January, however. This allows 
the BoJ a little more time to monitor the economy. Prime Minister Sanae Takaichi's 
government remains set on a domestic demand push, and we have a slight preference 
that the next two hikes are backloaded into January and April, right before the 
consumption tax rate on food is cut to 1% from 8%.

### Page 12

Monthly Economic Update October 2026
